---

# MNEE — Modular Networked Economic Engine

**MNEE** is a modular, product-oriented monorepo for building **programmable finance and portfolio-level economic engines** using smart contracts and deterministic policy enforcement.

This repository is structured as a **core + products** architecture:
- **Core** defines reusable, auditable primitives.
- **Products** compose the core into concrete financial runtimes.

The repo is intentionally **code-first, infrastructure-clean**, and suitable for long-term product evolution.

---

## Repository Structure

```
MNEE/
├─ core/                  # Shared, reusable economic primitives
│  ├─ solidity/            # Core Solidity contracts
│  ├─ js/                  # (future) shared JS/SDK logic
│  ├─ policy/              # (future) policy definitions
│  ├─ schemas/             # (future) data schemas
│  └─ README.md
│
├─ PFTA/                   # Product: Programmable Finance & Treasury Automation
│  ├─ contracts/
│  ├─ scripts/
│  ├─ test/
│  ├─ hardhat.config.js
│  └─ README.md
│
├─ PV/                     # Product: PortView (Portfolio-level kernel)
│  ├─ contracts/
│  │  └─ core/             # Vendored copy of core contracts (Hardhat constraint)
│  ├─ test/
│  ├─ hardhat.config.js
│  └─ README.md
│
├─ docs/                   # Architecture & product documentation
├─ bootstrap-core-and-pv.ps1
└─ .gitignore
```

---

## Design Principles

### 1. **Core / Product Separation**
- `core/` is **not a runnable app**.
- It contains **pure, reusable logic** intended to be embedded into products.
- Products **copy or vendor** core contracts as needed (Hardhat limitation: contracts must live inside the project).

### 2. **Deterministic Governance**
All economic actions are:
- Policy-checked
- Role-restricted
- Explicitly authorized

There is **no hidden automation** and no implicit privilege escalation.

### 3. **Enterprise-grade Repository Hygiene**
- `node_modules/`, build artifacts, and caches are **never committed**
- Repo history has been cleaned to remove accidental dependency commits
- Each product is independently installable and testable

---

## Core Module (`core/`)

The core defines **fundamental economic building blocks**, currently including:

### Solidity Contracts
- **TreasuryVault**
  - Custodies assets
  - Enforces admin-only authorization
  - Allows controlled release via authorized schedulers

- **PolicyEngine**
  - Defines payment limits and constraints
  - Admin-configurable governance layer

- **PaymentScheduler**
  - Executes time-based or condition-based payments
  - Enforced through Treasury + Policy

These contracts are **statelessly composable** and designed to be reused across multiple products.

> Note: Core contracts are vendored into each product’s `contracts/core/` folder due to Hardhat import constraints.

---

## Product: PFTA  
**Programmable Finance & Treasury Automation**

`PFTA/` is a **single-product runtime** focused on:
- Treasury custody
- Policy-controlled payments
- Scheduled execution

### Status
- Fully implemented
- Full integration test coverage
- Passes all Hardhat tests

### Run PFTA locally
```bash
cd PFTA
npm install
npx hardhat clean
npx hardhat compile
npx hardhat test
```

---

## Product: PV (PortView)  
**Portfolio-level Orchestration Kernel**

`PV/` composes the same core primitives into a **portfolio-level abstraction**.

Instead of managing a single treasury flow, PV:
- Instantiates its own policy, vault, and scheduler
- Acts as a **portfolio root** for higher-level orchestration

### Key Characteristics
- Explicit admin control
- No implicit initialization side effects
- Governance actions are **intentionally externalized**

### Run PV locally
```bash
cd PV
npm install
npx hardhat clean
npx hardhat compile
npx hardhat test
```

---

## Why Core Is Copied Into Products

Hardhat **does not allow imports outside a project root**.

To preserve:
- Clean product isolation
- Independent testing
- Deterministic builds

Each product **vendors** the required core contracts into:
```
contracts/core/
```

This is **by design**, not duplication by accident.

---

## Node & Tooling Notes

- Recommended Node version: **20+ (LTS)**
- Node 18 works but shows Hardhat warnings
- Hardhat is installed **locally per product**, not globally

---

## What This Repo Is (and Is Not)

### ✅ This repo *is*
- A real product codebase
- A reusable programmable-economics kernel
- Suitable for SaaS, infra, or protocol evolution

### ❌ This repo is *not*
- A demo or toy project
- A hackathon-only artifact
- A UI-focused application (yet)

---

## Roadmap (High-Level)

- Shared JS SDK over core contracts
- Policy schema formalization
- Multi-portfolio orchestration
- Off-chain indexers & analytics
- CI pipelines per product

---

- Add **architecture diagrams (textual or visual)**
- Prepare **versioning & release strategy**

You’ve done the hard part already — this repo is now **clean, sane, and extensible**.

```markdown
# MNEE — Modular, Networked, Enterprise Execution

MNEE is a modular, enterprise-grade execution framework for building multiple programmable finance and orchestration products inside a single governed monorepo.

This repository is **not a demo**, **not a PoC**, and **not hackathon-only code**.  
Each product is a real execution kernel with explicit authorization, deterministic behavior, and automated tests.

---

## Repository Structure

```
MNEE/
├── core/
│   ├── solidity/
│   ├── js/
│   ├── policy/
│   ├── schemas/
│   └── README.md
│
├── PFTA/
│   ├── contracts/
│   ├── scripts/
│   ├── test/
│   ├── hardhat.config.js
│   ├── package.json
│   └── README.md
│
├── PV/
│   ├── contracts/
│   ├── scripts/
│   ├── test/
│   ├── hardhat.config.js
│   ├── package.json
│   └── README.md
│
├── docs/
├── .gitignore
└── README.md
```

---

## Core Principles

- Deterministic execution only
- Explicit authorization in code
- No hidden side effects
- No shared mutable runtime state
- Product isolation by default
- Tests define correctness

Nothing runs implicitly.  
Nothing is auto-authorized.  
Nothing is coupled accidentally.

---

## core/ — Shared Execution Kernel

The `core` directory contains reusable, product-agnostic execution logic.

It includes:
- Treasury vault logic
- Policy enforcement engines
- Payment scheduling primitives
- Authorization patterns
- Shared interfaces

Rules:
- Core does not deploy independently
- Core has no environment config
- Core has no runtime ownership
- Products explicitly consume core logic

Core exists to reduce duplication, **not to centralize control**.

---

## Product: PFTA  
### Programmable Finance & Treasury Automation

PFTA is a treasury execution engine supporting:

- ERC-20 based treasury vaults
- Policy-bounded payments
- Time-scheduled execution
- Explicit admin and scheduler roles
- Deterministic fund release
- Full unit and integration tests

Run locally:

```bash
cd PFTA
npm install
npx hardhat compile
npx hardhat test
```

Status: **Stable and tested**

---

## Product: PV (PortView)  
### Portfolio-Level Orchestration

PV is a portfolio orchestration kernel built on MNEE core logic.

It composes:
- TreasuryVault
- PolicyEngine
- PaymentScheduler

into a single portfolio execution contract.

Characteristics:
- Explicit admin ownership
- No implicit initialization
- Authorization done deliberately
- No UI dependency
- Ready for multi-portfolio expansion

Run locally:

```bash
cd PV
npm install
npx hardhat compile
npx hardhat test
```

Status: **Stable and tested**

---

### 0110 · CHATGPT · 2026-01-13T01:24:54Z

Good catch — this is **not a logic or content problem**, it’s **a Markdown rendering boundary issue**. Here’s the precise reason and the fix.

---

## Why that section is **not rendering as Markdown**

That block **is Markdown**, but GitHub is treating part of it as **plain text** because of **one of these exact causes** (very common):

### 1️⃣ An unclosed code fence **before** this section  
If **any earlier** section has:

````
```bash
...
```

but **one backtick is missing or mismatched**, GitHub switches into *verbatim mode* and **everything after renders as plain text**.

✅ **This is the #1 cause** of what you’re seeing.

---

### 2️⃣ Invisible indentation (tabs or 4 spaces)
Markdown rule:
- **4 leading spaces = code block**

If this section is indented (even once), GitHub renders it as text.

This often happens when:
- Content is pasted from ChatGPT UI
- Copied from a rendered block instead of raw markdown
- Auto-indent kicks in

---

### 3️⃣ Mixed smart quotes or Unicode bullets
Less common, but:
- `•` instead of `-`
- Non-breaking spaces
can break list parsing.

---

## How to FIX it (100% reliable)

### ✅ Step 1: Open README.md in **raw edit mode** on GitHub
Click ✏️ **Edit**, not Preview.

---

### ✅ Step 2: Check the section **above** `Product: PV (PortView)`
Make sure **every** code block is closed:

❌ Bad (breaks everything after):
```
```bash
cd PV
npm install
``   ← missing one backtick
```

✅ Good:
```bash
cd PV
npm install
```

---

### ✅ Step 3: Force-reset the PV section formatting  
Replace the PV section with **this exact block** (no indentation, copy-paste as-is):

```markdown
## Product: PV (PortView)

### Portfolio-Level Orchestration

PV is a portfolio orchestration kernel built on MNEE core logic.

It composes:

- PaymentScheduler

into a single portfolio execution contract.

- No UI dependency
- Ready for multi-portfolio expansion

#### Run locally

```bash

npm install
npx hardhat compile
npx hardhat test
```

**Status:** Stable and tested
```

---

## Why I didn’t “force Markdown” earlier
Because:
- Markdown **was correct**
- GitHub rendering failed due to a **structural break earlier**
- Rewriting just this section wouldn’t fix the root cause

You were right to question it 👍

---

## Quick sanity check
After fixing:
- Headings should be bold + larger
- Bullets should align
- Code block should be grey boxed
- No monospaced paragraphs

- Explicit authorization patterns
- Shared interfaces

- Core does **not** deploy independently
- Core has **no environment config**
- Core has **no product assumptions**
- Products explicitly consume core logic
- Core exists to **centralize control and reduce duplication**

- Authorization done deliberately
